 Tool B (3:29)
 Review: For his third so release on the Mudline label he established back in 2024, Leo Gibbon has chosen to deliver a "six-track suite" of sample-driven cuts reportedly "pieced together from a balcony while travelling". It certainly sounds sunny and summery, with Gibbon variously blending mid-80s Mediterranean pop synths, evocative ambient chords, warming basslines, manipulated vocal samples, blazed post hip-hop beats, flashes of lifted instrumentation, effects-laden jazz guitar, immersive effects aplenty, tactile deep house grooves, otherworldly electronics and - on standout 'Chapel North' - the spoken rhymes of beat poet James Messiah. It's an impressive mini-album all told, and one tailor-made for relaxed moments and gentle, sun-splashed mornings. … Read more

 Butterfly Effect (11:47)
 Review: Since vinyl copies of Shinichi Atobe's debut album Butterfly Effect are still in demand, DDS has decided to offer-up a new clear vinyl pressing of the acclaimed set. It remains one of the mysterious, publicity-shy producer's most magnificent and fully-formed works: a singular musical vision that was released some 13 years after his then sole single, 2001's 'Ship-Scope'. Like much of Atobe's work, it offers a particularly atmospheric musical vision where ambient, off-kilter electronica, deep house and dub techno explorations come together to create one distinctive, otherworldly whole. Butterfly Effect has long been revered in certain circles and listening to this new pressing it's easy to see why. … Read more

 To Safety (1:27)
 Review: Joseph Bishara has spent the last decade and a half becoming one of horror's most reliable composers, working on the likes of Insidious, The Conjuring, Malignant and Annabelle. Hokum sees him deploying every trick he's learned for Damien McCarthy's film, starring Adam Scott as a grief-stricken author trapped in a remote Irish inn's supernatural mystery. It's a movie of patient, quiet dread and Bishara's score matches that with dissonant strings, unsettling electronics and choral vocals that build slowly rather than jump-scare. His background in sound design shows throughout with texture and score blurring together until the atmosphere feels utterly immersive and real. … Read more

 Mean Sine (10:43)
 Review: Lebanese-Canadian composer and sound artiste Radwan Ghazi Moumneh teams up with Polish experimental duo BNNT, employing a buzuk, modular synth, various electronic kit, live percussion and the kind of vocals that stay with you long into the night. A Faithful Daughter took four days to record, utilising the legendary Hotel2Tango studio in Montreal, where Moumneh's Jerusalem In My Heart project have been regular visitors. There's nothing particularly familiar here, though. The four-track EP is genuinely explorative, throwing us directly into a place of friction between Arabic musical traditions and outsider electronics. It's patient to the point of tension, and although clearly has elements that could be considered ambient-drone and electroacoustic, no label really fits the audio.

 Deine Reime Sind Schweine (5:43)
 Review: Some records belong to their era, others could be assigned to any and still make sense. German maverick DJ Koze aka Pampa Records boss Adolf Noise's Wo Die Rammelwolle Fliegt returns to vinyl with its unruly spirit fully intact and now given a sharper, fuller remaster that brings extra clarity to its eccentric sound. The accompanying 10" adds three more pieces, including the underground favourite 'Deine Reime sind Schweine (Regelrechte Schweine)', which makes the reissue particularly appealing to longtime followers. Crucially, the restoration doesn't sand away the character that made the original a cult release back in April 2005. … Read more

 Electric Forest (16:17)
 Review: The third and final part of American avant garde composer Jon Hassell's Pentimento trilogy captures his last ever concert, which was recorded in London in 2015 with Rick Cox and John von Seggern. Carefully edited and mixed by Jeff Rona, Music Is Invisible stretches well beyond a straight live document with themes from Listening To Pictures and Seeing Through Sound resurfacing inside these semi-improvised mutations. 'Blue Ba-Ya' is one such spectral reappearance, motifs drifting in and out like old brushstrokes under fresh paint. … Read more

 A Handful Of Dust (7:54)
 Review: Maria W Horn builds Melting Pillars of Snow like a poem, starting from its centre and radiating outward, which makes it quite disorienting at first. 'Scythe of Saturn' opens on dissonant, doom-laden ground with Michael Gira's dissonant wails before 'The Grand Oak/Tidens Tro,' distils everything into its clearest, most digestible form. Mattias Hallsten's sho, Anna Petrini's contrabass recorder and Mats Erlandsson's guitar move fluidly against Horn's own electronics throughout. The record's second half turns spectral with Horn's pitch-shifted voice whispering Sebald's own words over drones and growling synths. By closer 'A Handful of Dust,' the darkness lifts and distorted guitars give way to harp-like strums and choral voices. Quite the journey. … Read more

 Motive (4:31)
 Review: Another cracking open of the Short Span safe from expert Lancashire sound robber Miles Whittaker, 'Vault' being made up of precious items from inside that sonic lockbox. Better known as one half of Demdike Stare, plus Pendle Coven and Suum Cuique, he now compiles six warm-up jams pulled from 30 years of pre-session practice, never intended as finished pieces. The material here dates back to 1996, at which time he was attempting to recreate Basic Channel's 'Phylyps Trak' on a borrowed Korg X3 workstation and Ibanez PUE5 multi-pedal - we guess 'Homage' is the starter, being the one to sound most like that classic. … Read more

 GroomlakeRDcampout (3:26)
 Review: MPU101 is an enigmatic artist who has been tied to the cult Munich label Illian Tape for a while. The latest from the Poland-born, Bavaria-based producer is a glassy-eyed ambient album that is not polite background music, but more a world-building soundtrack. There are gentle MPC beats on occasion, swirling chords and pixelated pads, and plenty of forlorn moods. There is a fragility and vulnerability to much of the sounds that makes it feel like they were made on battered old hardware and dusty keyboards pulled from a storage facility that hasn't seen daylight in years. It's beguiling and beautiful and a great way to mentally escape. … Read more

 Flutter (8:00)
 Review: nthng is already well known for this meticulous productions that thread IDM, techno, dub and drum & bass and his fifth album, Last Light of the Sun, his debut on topo2, only backs up that reputation. It is a subtle listen for sinking into from the off. Tracks such as 'Orchid' capture that balance particularly well, while the closing 'Flutter' drifts into a spacious, almost dreamlike territory that recalls the melodic ambience of David August. There are moments when a little melancholy has a huge emotional impact, but there is also a strong refusal to overstate things. Last Light of the Sun is music that settles in and lingers. … Read more

 The Orb - "Phone Chat Line"
 Review: It was way back in 1996 that Dr Alex Paterson first decided to explore his vast catalogue of remixes as the Orb, offering up the seminal Auntie Aubrey Excursions compilation. A sequel arrived in 2001, but nothing since. Given that 25 years have passed, this third missive is well overdue. While there are some unreleased and extremely hard to find revisions from the 90s present - see the opening rub of Coldcut and Hex's 'Timber' - for the most part this is later period Orb, where the usual ambient house and ambient dub exploits sit side by side with dubby and minimal techno and plenty of pleasingly psychedelic workouts - remixes aplenty for Youth's Liquid Sound Design label, of course, but also rubs of old pals System 7, ambient survivor and Weatherall collaborator David Harrow, Francesco Lombardo and the Transit Kings (itself an Orb offshoot). … Read more

 The Orb - "Phone Chat Line"
 Review: Orb fans can rejoice because Auntie Aubrey's Part 3 revives the Orb Remix Project series after nearly two decades. It's a sprawling collection that spans three decades of Alex Paterson's playfully subversive remixes, from a melancholy rework of Transit Kings' 'The Last Lighthouse Keeper' - surely the only track to feature both The KLF's Jimmy Cauty AND Simon Day of The Fast Show fame - to System 7's sun-dappled 'Davy Jones' Locker' and Killing Joke's spy-thriller-esque 'European Super State,' featuring Youth on bass. Youth also teams up with Lx for a psychedelic cut fronted by Lara Smiles. Further contributions from Coldcut, David Harrow, Sendelica, Gaudi, DF Tram and more make this a collector's treasure trove whether you're an Orb devotee or serious ambient head … Read more

 Each Day, As If It Were Your Last - Faded Postcards & Tapes - A Reimagined Suite By Zake -
 Review: Pallette's Alexander Carlo Buckley moves past his debut's hypnotic, loop-based meditations into something far more restless here. Each Day As If It Were Your Last drifts through electronica, synthwave, lo-fi and ambient with beats surfacing beneath hazy synths and melodies that feel just out of reach. There is nostalgia baked in and worn soft at the edges. Buckley lets melancholy and lightness sit side by side, and the contrast is where the beauty lies. Yet another ambient masterclass from past Inside The Present that will soundtrack many of life's more introspective moments perfectly.

 Connection (6:33)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1135934-01-06-01.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1135934-01-06-01.mp3] 
 Reach Out (6:51)
 Review: Dutch collective and Throbbing Gristle/Psychic TV-affiliated Psychick Warriors Ov Gaia staked out their own corner of early 90s electronic music with Ov Biospheres And Sacred Grooves, first released in 1992 and now reissued by Forever Records in its complete form on vinyl for the first time. Working outside strict techno orthodoxy, Robbert Heynen, Reinier Brekelmans, Reinoud van den Broek and Tim Freeman twined tribal percussion, ambient drift, industrial texture and house momentum into a ritualistic long-form album, pulsing from 'The Challenge (Part One)' through 'Obsidian' to 'New Edge Mantra' with restless intent. Long hailed as a cult landmark of outsider Dutch electronica, the record is newly remastered with the band's approval, here on limited gatefold 2x12" (bundled in with a bonus 10" of previously unreleased tracks 'Connection' and 'Reach Out'). … Read more
 Played by: [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/chart/28484331] Alexis Le-Tan

 Weather Melody (4:16)
 Review: Over in New York, Emily A Sprague courts all sorts of praise with her latest generous 15-track album Cyano. A long threnody for Eurorack and voice, it contrasts Water Memory (2017) and her efforts as part of the Florist rather nicely, as she delves deeper into photographic concept, taking inspiration from botanical cyanotype pioneer Anna Atkins - like the UV-light printing method, the record unfolded quickly, imagining a day in the life of a character living on the planet Cyano. The trickling emo loop of 'Sing To' is by far the most infectious standout here, and it benefits well from Khotin's downbeat drum additions at the far end. … Read more
